- OpenCode writes credentials into files under the data root and the state root, and not the config root, which is where a reader looks first. This importer looked there too until the report said `no credentials here` on a machine that held two.
- `auth.json` is `path.join(Global.Path.data, "auth.json")` (`opencode/src/auth/index.ts:10`): an OAuth token per provider, written 0600 (`:88`). `mcp-auth.json` is `path.join(Global.Path.data, "mcp-auth.json")` (`opencode/src/mcp/auth.ts:37`), the MCP client's own OAuth cache, written the same way (`:80`).
- `password` is the CLI daemon's shared secret, at `path.join(directory, "password")` over `Global.Path.state` (`cli/src/services/daemon.ts:39-41`). It holds 32 random bytes as base64url (`:50`), written 0600 through a temp file and a rename (`:53-54`). So a reader never sees a half-written one. Its own comment gives the reason it is a file at all: so discovered clients can reconnect `"without exposing a password flag or environment variable"` (`:48-49`).
- `server.json` sits beside it and is deliberately not in this list. Its `Registration` is `{id, version?, url, pid}` (`cli/src/services/daemon.ts:23-28`): the address to reach the daemon and the pid of the one that runs it. It holds no secret. A credential label on it spends the one warning a user actually reads on a file with nothing in it.
- The contents of these files are the credentials of the user's accounts, so the importer reports their presence and nothing else. The only contact is `existsSync`: a name and a path, never a byte. Each name is looked for in the root its own source joins it to rather than in one root for all of them. A flat list sent `password` to the config root too, and found nothing.
- The read carries the root with each hit, because that is the part a report line has to get right. This probe is separate from the config-root pass that matches credential-shaped names. The probe reports a file whether or not any directory scan showed it. The config-root pass matches names with `(credential|secret|token|auth|\.env$)` and never opens any of them.
- Four tables of `opencode.db` hold credentials, and three of them hold nothing else. `account` carries `access_token` and `refresh_token`. `credential` and `control_account` are the machine's own stores. `session_share` has a `secret text notNull` column, the capability URL a shared session is behind. A migration reads the `session` and `message` tables and names these four.

**The instruction file, and the agents source**

- The instructions document is `<config>/AGENTS.md`, and it is not in conflict with the `agents` source. That source reads `~/.agents/AGENTS.md` (`agents-read.ts:36`), a different file in a different tree. This is OpenCode's own document, and the two are separate texts.

**The asset directories, and the two depths**

- Three kinds of asset sit under the config root, each under the tool's own spellings (`OPENCODE_ASSET_DIRS`). Skills live under `skill` and `skills`, agents under `agent`, `agents`, `mode` and `modes`, and commands under `command` and `commands`.
- The skill directories come from `{skill,skills}/**/SKILL.md` (`opencode/src/skill/index.ts:24`, scanned over every config directory at `:204-208`). V2 registers both of them unconditionally, as two separate sources (`packages/core/src/config/plugin/skill.ts:23-34`).
- The reader reads the agent directories at two different depths (`packages/core/src/config/plugin/agent.ts:21-24`): `agent` and `agents` recursively, `mode` and `modes` one level down. A nested `agent/build/plan.md` is a real agent to OpenCode and invisible to a one-level reader. `mode` and `modes` are v1's spelling of a primary agent, and v2 still reads them.
- The name v2 derives is the path relative to the config directory, with the first segment and the extension removed (`agent.ts:156-160`). So `agent/build/plan.md` is `build/plan`. The importer writes one file per agent under a flat directory, so the separator becomes a dash. `readCommandFiles` makes the same call for the same reason. `collectFileWrites` reports a collision between two flattened names instead of a silent win.
- A file under `mode` or `modes` is forced to `mode: "primary"` (`agent.ts:173`). Those two are worth a report line because the agent is offered to the session as a top-level choice, not spawned as a subagent.
- The extension stays in a flattened name, because `readMarkdownDir` put it there. That reader read one level and is what this replaces for `agent` and `agents`, and the target path is built from the name. A drop moves every agent file that was already migrated.
- A file whose frontmatter carries a `model:` gets a report note. The agent is copied verbatim, and the model resolves when a subagent starts. A name that no longer resolves falls back to the session model and says so.

**The skills, by OpenCode's own rule**

- OpenCode globs two shapes in a skill directory (`packages/core/src/skill.ts:79`): any `*.md` at the top level, and `SKILL.md` at any depth. It then names each hit (`skill.ts:87-99`). The name is the frontmatter's `name` if it has one, and otherwise the file's own basename. That fallback applies only when the file sits directly in the source directory.
- Two results follow, and both are silent in OpenCode. A bare `<dir>/<name>.md` is a skill, and a reader that only looks inside subdirectories for a `SKILL.md` never sees one. A `<dir>/<sub>/SKILL.md` whose frontmatter has no `name:` is not a skill at all. An import of it hands over something the user cannot run in OpenCode either. Those are returned as `unnamed` and named in the report.
- The name is the frontmatter's where there is one. So a skill can arrive under a different name than the directory it was found in. A nested skill carries the files beside its `SKILL.md`, and a bare top-level `.md` carries none. Its directory is the source directory, so anything scanned there belongs to some other skill too.
- A `.md` below the top that is not named `SKILL.md` is not a skill, and it is not counted either. OpenCode's glob does not match it, so nothing is lost by silence.
- The `skills` key has two shapes, v1's `{paths, urls}` and v2's one flat list. The two produce different report lines, because `skills.paths` and `skills.urls` name keys that do not exist in a v2 file.
- V2 drops the distinction, and the way it drops it is `[...(info.skills.paths ?? []), ...(info.skills.urls ?? [])]` (`core/src/v1/config/migrate.ts:62`). Paths come first, then URLs, with nothing at the boundary to mark which is which. The split here is made the way v2 itself makes it when it reads the list back (`config/plugin/skill.ts:35`). An entry is a URL when `URL.canParse` holds and its protocol is `http:` or `https:`, and a directory otherwise. A `git+ssh://` or `github:` entry is therefore a directory here as it is there. The report says the list was split, so a wrong call is a sentence the user can check. That beats a skill that silently did not import.
- Two more things v2 does with the same entries, and this importer does not. A relative path resolves against the workspace directory rather than the process's cwd (`config/plugin/skill.ts:43`), and `~/` expands against `global.home` (`:39`). The expansion matches, because the home is the process's own. The workspace-relative resolution has no equivalent in a migration that reads one user's home. So the report names those entries as paths that resolved to nothing.
- The vendor exclusion runs over the paths themselves rather than over the results. A `skills.paths` entry that points into `~/.claude` is caught the same way a scan of that tree is.
- The vendor trees are named by their presence under the home rather than by a scan. OpenCode harvests them whatever the config says, and the point of the report line is that the user learns another source owns them.

**The config root's own entries**

- The config root is listed once, with `withFileTypes`, rather than twice through `readDirectoryNames`, because that helper returns both kinds and the two answers then disagree. A `cache/` directory gets reported once as `N entries this importer reads nothing out of`, and again as a file with no map. That reads as two separate things wrong with a tree that has one thing in it.
- The credential names come out of the same pass, for the same reason. A directory called `tokens` is not a credential file. A name that says it is sends the user after a secret that is not there.
- There is no `isFile()` guard on the file side, because the `continue` above has already taken every directory out. A `Dirent` that is neither a directory nor a file (a symlink, a socket) is not a settings document under either spelling. An earlier guard read as the thing that kept directories out, when the `continue` is what does it. A test that mutates the guard turns nothing red, because the mutation changes no answer.
- An unreadable config root contributes nothing.
- The reader reads three files at the config root for nothing, with a reason each. `tui.json` and `tui.jsonc` hold the terminal UI's own settings: colours, keybinds and layout. `plugin-meta.json` holds install records: which plugin versions were fetched and how often they loaded.

**The roots, and the report's words for them**

- The rule that decided a root is rendered in the report's own words. The named cases are `$OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR` (the tool's own override, and it names the `opencode` directory itself), `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` + `/opencode`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME` + `/opencode` and `$XDG_STATE_HOME` + `/opencode`.
- The default case is worth spelling out rather than a bare path. On Windows the tree is at `~/.config/opencode` and not under `%APPDATA%`, because `xdg-basedir@5.1.0` has no Windows branch. A report that prints a bare path makes a reader at the usual place wonder whether the importer is broken.

## Shared architecture

Every source in this document runs the same pipeline. A home module names the data roots. A read module opens the documents and reads the sessions. A plan module turns the parsed data into labunbun settings, rules, skills, agents and MCP servers. The report names every value carried and every path left behind.

- A home module takes `home`, `cwd` and `env` as arguments, and never calls `os.homedir()`.
- The importer never writes into a source tree, and it opens an allow-list of paths rather than a whole root. A path outside the list is named, not opened.
- The report carries a credential path by name, and no reader opens it. The only contact is a check for existence, never a byte.
- A credential-shaped key is dropped before the plan runs, and the report names it. The scrub reads a fixed set of name-map keys (`packages/coding-agent/src/openclaw-read.ts:159`).
- A document that does not parse becomes a skip line with a reason. A broken file never aborts a run.
- Every reader returns its own parsed shape, and each shape keeps its skip list. `RawT3Code` and `readKimiCode` are two examples (`packages/coding-agent/src/t3-read.ts:98`, `packages/coding-agent/src/kimi-read.ts:247`).
- A session import runs in two phases. The list phase offers the candidates, and the read phase opens only the ones the user chose.
- A name collision gets a report line rather than a silent win by write order. The report names the copy that lost.
- An instruction document becomes a rule file. The shared helper is `planMemoryAsRule` (`packages/coding-agent/src/migrate-core.ts:781`).
- The plan writes settings, MCP servers, rules, skills, agents and history. Everything else is named in the report and not written. File writes go through `collectFileWrites` (`packages/coding-agent/src/migrate-core.ts:857`).
- A foreign mode under one name can do two jobs. The plan writes both halves of the pair rather than one.
- The walk is name-sorted, so two runs over one tree produce the same plan.
- A tree another source owns is named and not read. A second import of it puts one skill in twice.
- The report says what was not read, so a partial run is visible.

## Source map

Each row names the module files, the default root the product uses, and the artifacts the source keeps there. The modules sit in `packages/coding-agent/src/` under flat names, one group per source. Four sources have no `-session.ts` module: Cursor, Trae, T3 Code and OpenCode.

| Source | Modules | Default root | Main artifacts. |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Codewhale** | `codewhale-home.ts`, `codewhale-read.ts`, `codewhale-plan.ts`, `codewhale-session.ts`. | `CODEWHALE_HOME` wins when it holds an absolute path, else `~/.codewhale`, with the legacy `~/.deepseek` as a second root. | `config.toml`, `settings.toml`, permission rules, hooks, MCP servers, skills, instruction documents and sessions. |
| **MiMo Code** | `mimocode-home.ts`, `mimocode-read.ts`, `mimocode-plan.ts`, `mimocode-session.ts`. | `MIMOCODE_HOME` wins when it holds an absolute path, else the four XDG bases with `mimocode` appended. | three settings documents, asset trees, memory, instruction documents and the session database. |
| **Alma** | `alma-home.ts`, `alma-read.ts`, `alma-plan.ts`, `alma-session.ts`. | `~/.config/alma`, then the `userData` root, `~/.alma` and `~/alma`. | settings, identity documents, skills, MCP servers, hooks, memory and threads. |
| **Qoder** | `qoder-home.ts`, `qoder-read.ts`, `qoder-plan.ts`, `qoder-session.ts`. | `QODER_CONFIG_DIR` wins, else the `.qoder` name under the CLI home, or `.qoder-cn` for the China build. | three settings layers, MCP servers, hooks, skills, memory documents and `AGENTS.md`. |
| **Cursor** | `cursor-home.ts`, `cursor-read.ts`, `cursor-plan.ts`. | `$CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR`, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/cursor`, else `~/.cursor`. The data root is its own (`$CURSOR_DATA_DIR`, else `~/.cursor`). | `cli-config.json`, `hooks.json`, `mcp.json`, project rules, commands, agents, skills and the prompt list. |
| **Antigravity** | `antigravity-home.ts`, `antigravity-read.ts`, `antigravity-plan.ts`, `antigravity-session.ts`. | `~/.gemini/antigravity-ide` for the separately installed IDE, and the pre-split `~/.gemini/antigravity`. | the settings document, `mcp_config.json`, skills, workflows, memory documents and the conversations under `brain/`. |
| **OpenClaw** | `openclaw-home.ts`, `openclaw-read.ts`, `openclaw-plan.ts`, `openclaw-session.ts`. | `~/.openclaw`, the state directory with three spellings and one answer. | settings documents, MCP servers, hooks, managed skills, workspace documents and sessions. |
| **Trae** | `trae-home.ts`, `trae-read.ts`, `trae-plan.ts`. | `~/.trae`, or `~/.trae-cn` for the China build. The project tree is `<project>/.trae`. | `user_rules`, project rules, the two `mcp.json` files and the named asset trees. |
| **Grok Build** | `grok-home.ts`, `grok-read.ts`, `grok-plan.ts`, `grok-session.ts`. | `$GROK_HOME` when set, else `~/.grok`. | `config.toml`, plugins, skills, commands, agents, rules, sessions and prompt history. |
| **MiniMax Code** | `minimax-home.ts`, `minimax-read.ts`, `minimax-plan.ts`, `minimax-session.ts`. | `$MINIMAX_DATA_DIR` wins, else `$MAVIS_DATA_DIR`, else `<home>/.minimax`. | `config.yaml`, `permission.json`, the `mcp.json` files, skills, agents, `AGENTS.md` and sessions. |
| **T3 Code** | `t3-home.ts`, `t3-read.ts`, `t3-plan.ts`. | `$T3CODE_HOME`, else `<home>/.t3`, and the production tree lives under `userdata`. | the three state documents, and the threads and messages the state database holds. |
| **Step Code** | `step-home.ts`, `step-read.ts`, `step-plan.ts`, `step-session.ts`. | `STEPCODE_CONFIG_DIR`, else `<home>/.stepcode`, with `.step-harness` as the pre-rename tree. | `config.toml`, `auth.json`, `settings.json`, `models.json`, the asset trees and `agent/sessions`. |
| **Kimi Code** | `kimi-home.ts`, `kimi-read.ts`, `kimi-plan.ts`, `kimi-session.ts`. | `$KIMI_CODE_HOME`, whose `sessions/` tree holds each conversation. | `sessions/`, `agents/<agentId>/wire.jsonl`, `state.json` and `session_index.jsonl`. |
| **OpenCode** | `opencode-home.ts`, `opencode-read.ts`, `opencode-plan.ts`, `opencode-db.ts`. | `$OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR`, else the XDG config base plus `opencode`. On Windows the default is `~/.config/opencode`. | the three settings documents, `AGENTS.md`, the asset trees, `opencode.db` and the named credential files. |

## Coverage

The table gives, for each source, what the import carries into labunbun and what it only reports. A report line is not a loss. It tells the user what stayed in the source product.

| Source | Carried into labunbun | Reported, not read. |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Codewhale** | settings keys, permission rules, hooks, MCP servers, skills, instruction documents and sessions. | credential paths, the project settings document, the denylist names and the product's own importer. |
| **MiMo Code** | three settings documents, asset trees, memory, instruction documents and the session database. | the credential files, the managed configuration, the TUI document and the vendor trees. |
| **Alma** | settings, identity documents, skills, MCP servers, hooks, memory and threads. | the provider names, the credential surface and the skill roots it does not read. |
| **Qoder** | three settings layers, MCP servers, hooks, skills, memory documents and `AGENTS.md`. | the conversations (counted), the project `mcp.json`, the store this source does not open and every credential value. |
| **Cursor** | the CLI documents, project rules, commands, agents, skills and the prompt list. | the chat bodies in `state.vscdb`, the MCP OAuth tokens, the two decision lists and `pasted_text.json`. |
| **Antigravity** | the settings document, `mcp_config.json`, skills, workflows and memory documents. | the conversations (counted, never read), the OAuth tokens in the OS store and the trees named but not read. |
| **OpenClaw** | settings documents, MCP servers, hooks, managed skills, workspace documents and sessions. | the vendor trees and the credential-shaped keys inside the name maps. |
| **Trae** | the global and project rules and the two `mcp.json` files. | skills, commands, hooks, memory, the state database, the workspace storage and the cross-tool skills tree. |
| **Grok Build** | `config.toml`, plugins, skills, commands, agents, rules, sessions and prompt history. | workspace memory (counted), the machine-policy layers, the vendor trees and the plugin caches. |
| **MiniMax Code** | `config.yaml`, permission rules, the `mcp.json` files, skills, agents, `AGENTS.md` and sessions. | credentials, builtin skills and agents (counted), plans, drafts and the borrowed skill trees. |
| **T3 Code** | the three state documents, and the threads and messages from the projection tables. | the sensitive environment, the attachments and the per-project overrides. |
| **Step Code** | sessions from the canonical tree and the pre-rename `.step-harness` tree. | the directory it read, and every session-shaped file it left, each with a reason. |
| **Kimi Code** | the folded journal of the session's main agent. | child sessions, the other agents' journals, sessions with no transcript and every dropped part (each counted). |
| **OpenCode** | the three settings documents, the provider and MCP tables, `AGENTS.md`, skills, agents, commands and sessions from `opencode.db`. | the credential files, the four credential tables, `tui.json`, `plugin-meta.json` and the vendor trees. |
